Cover and NCDC colophon read directly. This booklet carries Topics 3–7, 10 and 11 of the Senior One course (Topics 1, 2, 8 and 9 are not in this book). Pages are cited by PDF page. PUPIL BOOK — its rows count in a lesson's taught-in figure.

**Book 3:** *Mathematics Teacher's Guide, Senior One* (prototype) (teacher's guide) — Uganda, Ministry of Education and Sports / NCDC, 2020; 2,380,656 bytes, 76 PDF pp., MD5 39c0a5387777e6ff0156427c92df4717. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — its rows are named in a lesson but counted in no taught-in figure; the matching Senior One prototype pupil textbook is not in hand (NCDC posted only this guide), and for the guide's Topics 1, 2, 8, 9 and 12 no Senior One pupil text is in hand at all.

## Samoa added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Samoa's whole in-hand Maths library from the PDFs — **9 files: 8 pupil textbooks + 1 curriculum statement, 0 teacher guides** — and the expansion map's “7 student Maths books, 0 teacher” count is **wrong**: the map missed the *Mathematics Year 12* typeset edition, staged in the records as a supposed workbook (“full-year-wk”) but proved from its title page and interior to be the complete MESC Year 12 pupil textbook (Units 1–8 + answers), and it missed the kind of the *Primary Curriculum Statement, Years 1–8*, which is a curriculum document, not a pupil book. The library's enumerated slot for the separate 113 MB scanned Year 12 edition is NOT in hand (live 404; only a truncated 1 MB archive stub exists) — that edition is cited nowhere and the slot stays a library-side matter; the complete typeset Year 12 pupil textbook in hand is the book read. No teacher guide for Maths exists in the Samoa set, and no Maths content was found hiding under another subject label. The eight pupil books are real pupil teaching books — lessons, worked examples and exercises addressed to the learner — so Samoa's pupil rows **count** in a lesson's taught-in figure. The curriculum statement (CMAD, MESC, January 2013) states what is REQUIRED to be taught in Samoa primary schools and teaches nothing itself: its rows are **curriculum evidence only** and count in no taught-in figure — Samoa has no primary pupil textbooks in hand, so every primary (Years 1–8) Samoa row in the course is curriculum evidence. Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching section of a pupil book (the book's own numbered sections and their named subsections), and one item per coded learning outcome (NR / PA / DA / MS / SG / WM codes) of the curriculum statement; worked examples and exercise sets inside a section are part of that section's item, not separate items. The books' own errors are taught corrected: Year 10 Book 2's §7.1 heading prints “Drawing an Englargement” (for Enlargement); Year 11 Book 2's unit opener prints “Dealing with Angles Larger than 900” (for 90°); Year 13's contents lists chapter 2 as “Linear Inequations” while the chapter's printed title is “Linear Equations” and it teaches both — placed under the printed title. Named exclusions, stocked nowhere: the answers sections of every pupil book; Year 13's “Problems and Investigations” end-of-chapter sections, its Glossary/Index and its formula Appendix; calculator-keystroke asides (the mathematics is placed, the keystrokes are not); the curriculum statement's strand progression tables (printed pp. 81–120), which restate its coded outcomes; and Year 12's Unit 8 project unit, placed as one item. Into this lesson the placements stock 13 items: 5 pupil + 8 curriculum evidence.

## Turkmenistan added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Turkmenistan's whole filed in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **5 pupil files = 3 pupil works, plus 1 teacher guide** (6 documents, 840 PDF pages in all) — and the expansion map's "3 student + 0 teacher" kind split is **wrong** (**flag F1**: the map counts one file per grade/track slot; it misses the second parts of both Turkmen works and the teacher guide entirely — any plan sized on "3 books" under-sizes the read by about 2.5×). The pupil works are the complete Turkmen-medium Grade 1 and Grade 2 courses — *Matematika* Parts 1 and 2 for each grade (Atabaýew & Öwezowa, 3rd editions, 2019/2020) — and Part 1 only of the Russian-medium Grade 1 course (Rulieva, Chernikova, Mavlyanova & Satdykova, 4th ed. 2022), a separate work by a separate author team, not a translation of the Turkmen pair; the two Grade 1 Turkmen books are scans with no text layer and were read from page renders. The one teacher book, N. Açilowa's *Başlangyç synplarda matematikany okatmagyň usulyýeti* (Methodology of Teaching Mathematics in the Primary Classes, Ylym, 2015), is a teacher-training study guide: it teaches NO pupil content, so its rows are **teacher evidence only** and count in no lesson's taught-in figure; the five pupil books are real pupil textbooks, recommended by the Ministry of Education of Turkmenistan and served by the Ministry's own E-kitaphana, so their rows **count**. **Flag F2** (record-side: reported here, not fixed; merges never edit the records, and no `maths/books/turkmenistan.md` filing exists): a `books.json` note claims the Russian Grade 1 Part 2 "is E-kitaphana post 507" — post 507 is in fact **Grade 2 Russian Mathematics Part 1** (per the enumeration table and the grid match), no Grade 1 Russian Part 2 post exists in the catalogue, and that Part 2 is recorded nowhere as found; post 507 must not be double-counted as both a Grade 1 and a Grade 2 book, and the Russian Grade 1 work stands in hand as Part 1 only. **Flag F3**: the filing notes for these books rested on samples ("every-fifth-page verification", contents lists not yet verified); this full re-read confirms the sampled claims spot-checked — e.g. the kilogram section of Grade 1 (Turkmen) Part 2 does sit at printed pp. 63–64 — and supplies the missing contents lists for both Grade 2 parts (Part 1 contents at PDF p. 103; Part 2 at PDF pp. 118–119); no sampled content claim checked was found false. **Flag F4**: the map's "2 link-only" figure cannot be confirmed and looks far too low — the banked E-kitaphana grid match carries Maths links for the Grade 2 Russian pair and for Grades 3–9 Mathematics, Algebra and Geometry, plus a Grade 5 methodology guide; every one of those is link-only, not in hand and cited nowhere, and everything beyond Grade 2 is outside this set. Byte-identical duplicate posts are cited once, on the filed copy (post 403 = the Grade 1 Part 2 file; post 412 = the Grade 2 Part 1 file; post 418 = the Grade 2 Part 2 file; post 576 = the teacher guide's post 555 file), and every pupil file was pulled from its GitHub release asset at the exact recorded byte size (reconcile sweep #8 lists 23/23 Turkmenistan entries secured). Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching section of a book (its contents-list section, or a headed teaching block where the contents list runs broader), with consolidation/revision sections counted once per book-part; pure exercise pages inside a section are apparatus of that section's item. In every book in this set the printed folio equals the PDF page. One reading note, carried exactly: in the Grade 1 (Turkmen) Part 1 book's type, the empty answer box prints as a small square — in the PDF font layer it can appear as «*» (e.g. the comparing exercises "7 * 6" mean "put <, > or = between 7 and 6") — it is NOT a multiplication sign, and multiplication is not taught in that book. Not stocked as separate rows, exactly as the placements direct: the Grade 2 Part 1 money item 14 (manat notes and coins inside addition/subtraction stories at printed pp. 50, 61 — context only, no money section is taught in that book, and the placements count it inside items 11–13), and the four consolidation/revision items the placements assign to no lesson (Grade 2 Part 1 item 17, Grade 2 Part 2 item 42, Grade 1 Part 1 item 71, Grade 1 Russian Part 1 item 125) — practice over sections already stocked, no new content. Turkmenistan's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** on the as-filed set. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 item, all of it teacher evidence.

**Book 1:** *Başlangyç synplarda matematikany okatmagyň usulyýeti* [Methodology of Teaching Mathematics in the Primary Classes] (teacher-training study guide) — N. Açilowa, Aşgabat: Ylym, 2015; recommended by the Ministry of Education of Turkmenistan; a study-methodology guide for higher and secondary pedagogical schools; Ministry E-kitaphana post 555 (post 576 is the same file, cited once); 4,173,475 bytes, 272 PDF pp.; read from the copy held in the E-kitaphana work folder at the exact recorded byte size. It teaches NO pupil content. Printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — its rows are named in a lesson but counted in no taught-in figure.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Fractions in the primary course: first the share/part of a whole, then the fraction concept itself | Turkmenistan — *Başlangyç synplarda matematikany okatmagyň usulyýeti* (teacher-training methodology guide), Ch. X, printed pp. 237–244 — teacher evidence |

Turkmenistan is added to the countries line as teacher evidence only — every Turkmenistan row in this lesson comes from the Açilowa methodology guide, which teaches no pupil content, so Turkmenistan is named here but added to no acceptance count (the Ghana teacher-evidence precedent). Turkmenistan's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** on the as-filed set. Flags F1–F4 are library-record matters — reported here, not fixed by this merge.

## Tunisia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Tunisia's whole in-hand Maths set from the file — exactly 1 book, a teacher's guide, and no pupil textbook: the *Mathematics — Teacher's Book, Year 1 of Basic Education* (CNP code 502107P00). The expansion map is CORRECT for the in-hand set (0 student + 1 teacher), with one title refinement carried everywhere: the library/checklist title says “grades 1–2” because the checklist's one teacher-guide row spans both volumes, but this file is the **Year 1 volume only** — its cover names Year 1 alone, and the Year 2 volume is the separate file 502205P00. The guide is the only mathematics row for Tunisia in `textbooks.tsv` (75 Tunisia rows: Arabic, English, French, philosophy, Spanish and this one) and the only mathematics entry in any staging `books.json`; no Maths hides under another subject label and there is no “all subjects” Tunisia volume. The book was grabbed byte-exact through the authenticated GitHub API (9,205,715 bytes, 184 PDF pages, MD5 d017ae08ae7c35d335757b0fb1ef747f — matching the library's verified record for the complete Wayback capture of 502107P00; staging holds no Tunisia PDF on disk) and read in full from rendered pages — the PDF's text layer is glyph-encoded (extraction is mojibake) and Arabic OCR returned nothing on these watermarked scans; the diagonal CNP watermark does not hide body text. Printed folio = PDF page throughout. Counting grain: one item per distinct concept, skill or teaching point the guide itself teaches or explains, at the grain the guide presents it; lesson-plan conduct prose, activity scripts and assessment apparatus are not separate items unless they carry a distinct mathematical teaching point. Where the guide itself separates teacher background theory from what Year 1 pupils actually do, the item says so — the teacher-background items (16, 17, 28, 57, 58, 75, 76 and 77) are placed as teacher background, exactly as the placements word them. The placements' named exclusions are stocked nowhere: the life-skills chapter (printed pp. 55–67 — general life-skills/21st-century-skills pedagogy, no mathematics content); the error-remediation procedure, didactic-contract discussion and remediation planning (printed pp. 45–50 passim — teaching apparatus; the content-bearing error examples are placed as items); the model lesson plans (printed pp. 78–132) beyond the points placed from the distributed sequence; and the pupil-facing integration and problem-solving worksheets of Part Three (printed pp. 158–179), practice material enacting the placed concepts and introducing none the reference or sequence sections do not already teach. Record items R1–R5 are apparatus or exam evidence, stocked nowhere as teaching items: R1 the assessment typology — diagnostic before new learning, formative during, certifying at a period's or year's end (printed pp. 41–42); R2 the six period evaluations (printed pp. 134–152) — exam evidence only; R3 the closing Arabic–French glossary of mathematical terms (printed pp. 154–157); R4 the guide's statement (printed p. 158) that the pupil textbook is held to at most 120 pages, so the extra integration and problem-solving situations are printed in this guide instead — the pupil book and this guide are designed as one package; R5 the bibliography (printed pp. 180–182). Set boundary: the pupil textbooks for Years 1, 2 and 6 (102105P01, 102105P02, 102210P00, 102609P00), the Year 2 volume of this teacher's guide (502205P00) and all later-year najahni/exercice.tn material are exact-link only — recorded links, never downloaded, in no `books.json`, not in hand and not read. Tunisia is **TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY** in every lesson: every item is worded as what the guide teaches or explains, Tunisia is named in the countries line and in the rows below, and it is added to NO lesson's acceptance count, on the Ghana precedent. Tunisia's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (teacher-evidence-only)**. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 teacher-evidence items from Book 1.

## Trinidad and Tobago added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Trinidad and Tobago's whole in-hand Maths-bearing set from the files — **9 files, all curriculum/teacher evidence, 0 pupil textbooks**: the dedicated *Primary School Curriculum — Curriculum Guides: Mathematics (Infant 1 – Standard 5)* (MoE, Curriculum Planning and Development Division, 2013), read in full; seven per-level all-subject Primary Curriculum Guides (Infants 1, Infants 2, Standards 1–5), recorded in every record under the plain “Curriculum Guide” label, whose Mathematics sections are the **same curriculum text** as the dedicated guide's section for that level — verified file by file (normalised-text sequence similarity 0.9973 Infants 1, 0.9977 Infants 2, 0.9975 Standard 2, 0.9986 Standard 5; word-multiset containment 1.0000 Standard 1 and 0.9948 Standard 4, whose per-level productions extract in a different column order; Standard 3's scanned guide verified by OCR — rationale “What Is Mathematics About?” at PDF p. 60, “MATHEMATICS: STANDARD 3” tables at PDF pp. 70/80/90, three content spot-checks matching) — so they are **cited once, through the dedicated guide** (placements Record items R1–R7), never double-counted; and the *National Early Childhood Care and Education Curriculum Guide* (ages 3–4), whose mathematics strand is placed separately (items 245–249) because the dedicated guide starts at Infant 1. The expansion map's set picture (0 student + 1 teacher) is wrong as a set description: the dedicated guide it counted is real, but the true in-hand set is these 9 files, and **no pupil Maths textbook for Trinidad and Tobago exists in hand under any label** (all pupil textbooks are commercial — STP Caribbean Mathematics and others; none is held). Every document in the set is a Ministry curriculum guide — a document that states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself — so every Trinidad and Tobago item is a curriculum REQUIREMENT item, worded as curriculum evidence. Counting grain: one item per numbered Outcome in the guide's OUTCOMES column, with the placements' stated folds (a bare “solve problems involving X” outcome that restates its own section is folded into that section's item; per-measure vocabulary outcomes are folded into their measure's item; each strand's closing Language outcome is its own item). The placements' named exclusions are stocked nowhere: the dedicated guide's front matter (PDF pp. 1–26 — contents, National Curriculum Framework prose, Subject Rationale); the Dispositions columns of every table (attitudes and values — apparatus, never items); the per-level guides' non-Mathematics subjects; the 17 Forms 1–3 guides staged at grade-08 (ELA, ICT, Spanish, Integrated Science, Social Sciences, VAPA, Visual Arts, Dance, Drama, Music, PE, HFLE, Technology Education — checked book by book; none is a Mathematics book; the Social Sciences staging anchor was labelled “Teacher's Guide” in its source bundle, but the file itself is the Social Sciences Curriculum Guide — a records mislabel, noted, not a Maths item); and the ECCE guide's non-mathematics strands and apparatus-level mentions (§5.9.9 equipment list; §5.9.16 a number line 1–10 in a materials list). Book errors are read corrected: in Standard 4's Number Patterns table the Outcomes-column wording of Outcome 5 is displaced across Outcomes 4 and 6 in print — the requirement was read from its elaborations (the items 183/184 boundary) and is placed corrected; a Standard 4 Decimals conversion elaboration cross-cites code [1.1.5] where [1.1.15] is meant, read as 1.1.15; and fraction glyphs (halves, quarters, fifths, tenths) are dropped at several points in the dedicated guide's text layer — the fractions were read from context and the surrounding elaborations. Set boundary: the *NCSE Mathematics Curriculum Guide (Forms 1–3)* is **link-only, not in hand** — exact links known (official MoE blob copy 2,900,122 B; IBE/UNESCO mirror 2,587,043 B) but the file was never staged or downloaded, so it was not read and is cited nowhere; the NCSE Mathematics *Teacher's* Guide has no public PDF on any route checked by the library fronts (provisional negative). | The Primary Mathematics guide requires proper fractions, improper fractions and mixed numbers, and converting between them | Trinidad and Tobago — *Primary Mathematics Curriculum Guide (Infant 1 – Standard 5)* (curriculum guide) — curriculum evidence, printed p. 176 |

Trinidad and Tobago enters this lesson as curriculum evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — no Trinidad and Tobago pupil Maths textbook is in hand; the whole in-hand set is Ministry curriculum guides, which state requirements without teaching (the standing Barbados/Ghana evidence precedent). Trinidad and Tobago's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): every in-hand Maths-bearing file is read and stocked, and it advances only when a Trinidad and Tobago pupil textbook lands in hand and is read, and when the link-only NCSE Mathematics Curriculum Guide (Forms 1–3) is secured and read.

## Zimbabwe added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Zimbabwe's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 files, all teacher's guides, 0 pupil books**: the Secondary Book Press *PlusOne* (Heritage-Based Curriculum) Mathematics Teacher's Guides for ECD A, ECD B and Grades 1–7. The expansion map is CORRECT for Zimbabwe Maths (0 student + 9 teacher) — no correction. Every file was grabbed from GitHub via the authenticated API (the publisher source URLs returned a Cloudflare challenge page, not the PDFs — a route failure, recorded as placements note N2 and not used) and matches its staging `books.json` record on byte size, SHA-256 and pdfinfo page count exactly (745 PDF pages in total); title/imprint pages were read directly, and contents-against-pages completeness was checked for every book — all 9 are COMPLETE full guides, no sampler or extract among them. No Maths content hides under another subject label: the other 42 in-hand Zimbabwe files are the same PlusOne TG series in English, ChiShona, Science and Technology, Social Science and Physical Education and Arts — none is an "all subjects" volume and none carries Maths units. The ZIMSAKE learner's books once counted were proved publisher SAMPLERS and removed in 2026-11 — 8 of 8 ZIMSAKE files ever pulled are proven samplers, none is a Maths book and none is in hand; the Grade 7 Mathematics *Revision Guide* is not in hand and is an excluded class in any case (revision guides do not count). Counting grain: one item per contents sub-lesson (ECD guides, whose units are sequences of workbook-page lessons) or per numbered unit (Grades 1–7), split only where one unit plainly teaches two distinct course lessons. Pages cited are the books' printed pages (printed folio = PDF page − 4 in the ECD guides; Grades 1–7 unit starts are the contents printed pages, verified against the folios). Book errors found on the read are taught corrected and recorded: the Grade 4 contents lists Unit 17 Money at printed p. 59 and Unit 18 Time at p. 58 — the page order in the contents is reversed; the units themselves run Money (objectives printed p. 56) before Time, and the Money placement cites the objectives page; the Grade 6 Unit 23 (Time) guide cites its Learner's Book as "pages 18−204" — a dropped digit (the unit's own exercises sit at Learner's Book pp. 189–204), a citation slip in the guide that does not affect the teaching content; and the Grade 7 contents misnumbers the final Data handling unit as "Unit 21" although it follows Unit 30 — the body heads it Unit 31, and the placement records both numbers. The Grade 4 Unit 5 decimal comma (3,1 for three-and-a-tenth) is the book's consistent notation, recorded as printed, not treated as an error; decimals are written with the decimal comma throughout Grades 4–7. Record note N1: `phase3-push-state.json` still lists only 4 Zimbabwe folders (paperwork) although all 51 Zimbabwe files — including these 9 — are confirmed live on GitHub (sweep #8, 2026-11-05); the state-file gap is already on record, and this read re-confirms the 9 Maths files on GitHub at exact recorded sizes and hashes. Zimbabwe is **TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY** in every lesson: every item is worded as what the guide states the teacher teaches and what learners should be able to do, Zimbabwe is named in the countries line and in the rows below, and it is added to NO lesson's acceptance count, on the Ghana precedent. Zimbabwe's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (evidence-only)**. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 teacher-evidence item(s).

## Uruguay added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Uruguay's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books: five pupil WORKBOOKS of the official series *Cuaderno para Hacer Matemática* (ANEP · DGEIP · CACEEM) — Inicial, Primero (5th ed. 2021), Segundo, Tercero and Quinto (5th ed. 2021) — plus one teacher book, *Matemática en el Segundo Ciclo — Libro para el Maestro* (Grades 4–6)** — and placed 340 items (304 pupil + 36 teacher-evidence). The expansion map's count is right and its kind label is wrong (flag F1 — record-side, reported here, not fixed by this merge; merges never edit the filings or the records): the five pupil books are activity **workbooks**, not student textbooks. A workbook is a pupil book (the South Africa/Peru pattern): the pupil-workbook items below are **teaching content**, so Uruguay's pupil rows are COUNTED in full. The teacher book's items are **teacher evidence only** — worded as teacher evidence, named in the countries line and in the rows, and added to no acceptance count (the Ghana precedent); the teacher book is also the only in-hand coverage of the Grade 4 and Grade 6 pupil content, whose workbooks are not in hand. **Not in hand, not read, cited nowhere**: the Grade 4 (Cuarto) pupil workbook (36,381,739 B) and the Grade 6 (Sexto) pupil workbook (50,368,322 B) — official DGEIP URLs banked, but every banked Wayback capture is crawler-truncated at 5,242,880 B, the live DGEIP host does not answer from this network, and the Grade 4 book also circulates only as a login-gated Studocu copy (not pursued, per the no-login rule) — and the Primer Ciclo teacher book (Inicial–Grade 3, 5,703,330 B), likewise link-only (captures truncated at 1,048,576 B). Record flag F2 (record-side, reported, not fixed): `textbooks.tsv` carries no row for the Grade 3 workbook, although `phase3-push-state.json` records it as pushed and its staging folder carries a books.json — the book is in hand and was read in full. The Grade 5 workbook is an image-only PDF and was read from page renders; the other five books were read from their text layers. Book errors found on the read are taught corrected: the Grade 3 workbook's garden table (printed p. 79) prints last year's figures 40, 35 and 20 with a total of 85 — the rows sum to 95; the Grade 5 workbook's worked example (printed p. 84) decomposes 0.65 as 6/10 + 9/100, which makes 0.69 — corrected to 6/10 + 5/100 (the decimal form printed beside it, 0.65 = 0.6 + 0.05, is correct); and the Grade 5 book's printed p. 14 side tab names the wrong guide character, "Lechuza" for this book's "Yacaré" — a production error only, content unaffected. (The Grade 5 book's p. 116 "almost two and a half times more casein" line is NOT an error: it is a pupil claim the book itself asks children to judge, and it is placed as such.) The placements' named exclusions are apparatus, stocked nowhere: the workbooks' cut-out pages (recortables), the Inicial personal pages (printed pp. 8–9) and its ice-melting observation page (printed p. 78 — science, no maths content), and the Grade 5 opening questionnaire, guide-character pages and recortables. Printed-page basis: printed page = PDF page − 2 in all six books. Counting grain: one item per distinct activity / teaching point in a workbook; one item per distinct teaching guidance / content statement in the teacher book. Into this lesson the placements stock 25 items — 22 pupil-workbook rows and 3 teacher-evidence rows.
